2026.11.09 晚报

覆盖窗口：2026-11-08 21:30:00 至 2026-11-09 21:30:00 北京时间 · 更新时间：2026.11.09 21:50 北京时间 · 253 条

窗口：2026-11-08 21:30 至 2026-11-09 21:30（北京时间，UTC+8）｜监控名单 134 个账号｜所有时间均为北京时间。

Executive Summary｜核心结论

本期窗口为 2026-11-08 21:30 至 2026-11-09 21:30（北京时间，UTC+8），覆盖周日夜间至周一亚洲交易日。主线高度集中：Nvidia / 黄仁勋“中国间谍”事件从突发新闻演变为董事会独立调查 + 国会三委员会约见，市场与观点分化；AI 基础设施“谁在承担融资风险”被 CDS 与担保数据拆解；存储/半导体设备链条出现 EUV 争夺、Terafab 巨量产能目标、Lam Research 泄密等新变量；模型竞赛方面 GPT-6.2、Claude 5.6、Gemini Krypton 等传闻密集；Anthropic 据报于周一在下跌市场中启动 IPO 推介。注意：本窗口前 8.5 小时（21:30–06:00）与今日早报窗口重叠，少量帖子两期均有收录。以下“分析”为本摘要的综合判断；凡标注“据称/传闻/作者观点”的内容均来自 X 帖子，未经独立核实。

一、Nvidia：关键人物 + 地缘政治风险进入“程序化”阶段（董事会调查、国会约见）

事实层（媒体转述）：@markgurman 转述 Bloomberg 称黄仁勋承认婚外情、董事会调查其是否成为中国间谍行动目标；@dnystedt、@Omercheema 称涉事女子为曾转运 Nvidia 芯片的新加坡公司 Megaspeed 前高管；@GavinSBaker 转述董事会声明：无理由相信国家安全或公司机密被泄露，启动由独立董事委员会监督、外部律师协助的独立调查，黄仁勋表示全力配合；@AndrewCurran_ 称其被要求会见众议院外交、对华特别委员会、情报委员会主席。

观点分化：@dylan522p 认为未来 6 个月媒体/金融圈会平息，但民主党会借国会听证彻查对华 GPU 销售——真正的风险在那时；@jukan05 转述陆行之（Andrew Lu）认为这是美方借丑闻推进三大目标（削弱其反制裁游说、收紧出口/境外租用等）；@teortaxesTex 认为 MSS 这盘棋下得“近乎最优”；@GavinSBaker 为其“美国最先、最多、最好 + 只给中国降规版”的政策立场辩护。市场端：@aleabitoreddit 称 TAIEX 周一 +4.94%（史上第二大单日涨幅），@benitoz 转述 Bernstein Stacy Rasgon 认为是噪音而非基本面问题。

我们的分析：事件从“个人丑闻”转为“对华敞口审查”。对 NVDA 的基本面风险不在 CEO 去留，而在于中国相关需求——@zephyr_z9 估计合法销售 + 境外租用 + 走私合计约占 Nvidia 算力需求 ~20%，且称约 60 万颗 H200 滞留香港——在国会程序中被进一步压缩，以及禁止中国公司境外租用算力对 GPU 现货/neocloud 租赁价格的冲击。需要跟踪：听证时间表、境外租用（Malaysia/Singapore）规则、H200 对华许可。

二、AI 基础设施：需求仍强，但融资风险在“提供方”与“买方”之间重新定价

@nodiligence：Anthropic 以 34 亿美元买下 1.7GW Caterpillar 燃气发电机（约 2,000 美元/kW 含安装，买的是排队位置），按 GB300 ~4 美元/小时，这些电力约可支撑每月 ~20 亿美元算力——电力相对芯片“便宜到不计代价”；Nebius 新合同 1.8–2.8 年收入即可收回建设成本。

@nodiligence：民主党拿下国会两天后 SpaceX、CoreWeave CDS 创纪录，而大型 hyperscaler 保护成本反而下降；Broadcom 为 Anthropic 芯片融资提供约 720 亿美元担保，AMD 合作伙伴数据中心担保一季度内从 41 亿升至 77 亿美元；路透引 UBS 估计 2028 年美国计划中约 30% 数据中心容量可能暂停。@semivision_tw 转述 CNN：支持暂停建数据中心的民主党州长当选人所在州占全美规划容量约 45%（德州 25%）。

@FundaAI 周报：长期对 AGI（2028 或之后）更乐观，但中期（至 2027）更难判断，收入集中于少数实验室；@aleabitoreddit 汇总 LITE/AMD/ARM/Meta 等管理层“需求订单簿与股价脱节”的表态。

我们的分析：市场正在区分“现金流付费方”（hyperscaler）与“资产负债表承担方”（neocloud、芯片厂担保、SPV）。对 AVGO/AMD 而言，担保规模是新的尾部风险变量；对 neocloud，短回收期支撑信用，但政策（州级暂停）是边际最大的不确定性。电力/现场发电（CAT、GEV、BE）受益逻辑未变。

三、存储与半导体设备：供给约束更硬，竞争格局出现新变量

@dnystedt：ASML 成为 DRAM“造王者”，三星、SK 海力士各投 10–20 万亿韩元买 EUV、交期最长翻倍，ASML 称 2027–28 供应计划无法满足需求；@jukan05 转述日经：Terafab 目标 2032 年起 100 万片/月（70 万片存储），@SemiconductorsX 对比三星 2027 年约 85.5 万片/月。

@jukan05：韩媒称 Lam Research 韩国公司涉嫌泄露三星蚀刻技术机密，可能需赔偿约 1 万亿韩元，三星在权衡行动；@dnystedt：三井化学以 1.84 万亿日元收购 JSR（光刻胶），股价盘中跌约 13%；@zephyr_z9：海力士 Advanced MR-MUF 问题拖累 HBM4 良率与 Rubin 爬坡，长鑫（CXMT）本月或下月 HBM 量产。

关键词扫描：JPM 称存储龙头远期 P/E 约 3.5x、2026–28 利润近每年翻倍（转述）；SK 海力士在进博会称紧缺持续到 2028 年；@MarcelLarsen_ 长文论证存储 LTA（浮动价 + 底价 + 现金押金/照付不议）结构远强于 onsemi/Microchip 被违约的合同。

我们的分析：存储“周期见顶”争论中，供给侧（EUV 交期、HBM 良率、OSAT 外包）证据偏多头；Lam 泄密若坐实，可能影响三星蚀刻设备份额分配（利好 TEL / 韩国本土设备商），值得跟踪。

四、模型竞赛与 Anthropic IPO：发布节奏加快，资本市场窗口不佳

传闻层：@TokenGremlin 称 GPT-6.2 Astra 测试完成、Sol 最终测试，Anthropic Sonnet/Opus 5.6 已在 Claude Code 中 A/B；@chetaslua、@kimmonismus 测试/转述 Google Gemini Krypton 输出，称 Google“回来了”；@AndrewCurran_ 称 OpenAI 内部模型（GPT-Aeon）测试时算力未见收益递减，DeepSeek Harness 不到 3 个月达 154 万 DAU。

关键词扫描：Bloomberg 称 Anthropic 周一起在下跌市场中推介 IPO（价格区间未公开）；JarsyInc 周报汇总 Anthropic 修订 S-1 显示 Q3 收入 169.6 亿美元、基石投资者推 1.5–1.6 万亿美元估值 vs 部分投资者认为应接近 1 万亿；a16z/Ornn 数据称 5 月以来 token 混合价格 −67% 而支出 +122%（Ramp）。

我们的分析：模型迭代周期已缩短到 60–90 天，Google 若凭算力优势追上，定价压力将从开源权重扩散到前沿闭源；Anthropic IPO 定价将成为 AI 私募估值的锚，下跌市场中的发行折价会传导到 OpenAI 及二级 AI 软件估值。

五、应用层与其他：Apple 加速 agentic Siri，SoftBank 周二财报

@markgurman Power On：Apple 最早明年推出主动执行任务的 agentic Siri，CEO John Ternus 敦促 Federighi 加速 AI 路线图，Mike Rockwell 可能卸任 Siri 负责人；@swyx 称 Cursor 2025 年底 ARR 10 亿美元。

关键词扫描：SoftBank 11 月 10 日发布财报（上周五 −10.8%），关注 OpenAI 300 亿美元承诺兑现与 LTV；Bloom Energy 尽管宣布 Google/NTT DATA 项目仍一度 −16%，NZS Capital Q3 新建 BE 仓位。

Highlights / Top 10｜按投资重要性排序

#1 @GavinSBaker · 2026-11-09 08:41 北京时间

要点：Nvidia 董事会今天表示，没有理由相信 Jensen 的行为导致国家安全或 Nvidia 机密遭到泄露。

他们正在启动一项由独立董事组成的委员会监督、并由外部独立法律顾问协助的独立调查，将审查所有可获得的信息。鉴于这些独立董事负有的受托责任，这将是一项公正、详尽而彻底的调查。

Jensen 表示：“我支持这项调查，并将全力配合。我热爱我们的国家和我们的公司。我致力于保护 NVIDIA 并支持美国的国家安全。我为身为美国人感到自豪和感激。”

我认识 Jensen 将近 30 年了。我根本不相信他会故意做任何对美国或 Nvidia 不利的事情。

他对我谈到中国时唯一说过的，就是保持以 Nvidia 为首的美国硬件生态领先于中国的绝对重要性。仅此而已。

更详细的想法见下文：

为何重要：董事会正式启动独立调查并给出“无泄密证据”的初步表态，决定了周一 NVDA 的定价框架；Gavin Baker 是最有分量的多头声音之一。

#5 @nodiligence · 2026-11-09 20:16 北京时间

要点：$AVGO $AMD $MSFT $ORCL $SPCX $CRWV $META $GOOGL

民主党拿下国会两天后，SpaceX 和 CoreWeave 的 CDS 创下纪录，而 Bloomberg 报道称大型超大规模云厂商的保护成本实际上变便宜了。所以市场已经在把为 AI 建设买单的公司与为其提供融资的公司区分开来。

看看现在谁在持有风险，这就说得通了。

—— Broadcom 为 Anthropic 的芯片融资提供了约 720 亿美元的担保，
—— AMD 对合作伙伴数据中心的担保在一个季度内从 41 亿美元升至 77 亿美元。

路透引用 UBS 的估计称，美国计划于 2028 年投产的数据中心容量中约 30% 可能被暂停。

如果发生这种情况，我认为 Microsoft 或 Meta 只是少花点……（全文见下方分作者部分）

为何重要：CDS 与担保数据显示 AI 建设的融资风险集中在 neocloud 与芯片厂担保（AVGO 720 亿、AMD 77 亿），而非 hyperscaler。

#6 @nodiligence · 2026-11-09 18:05 北京时间

要点：对于一家 AI 实验室来说，燃气发电机与它所启动的芯片相比如此便宜，以至于它愿意付出任何代价尽早得到一台。

Anthropic 花 34 亿美元买下了 1.7GW 的 Caterpillar 发电机，这些是圣安东尼奥的电力开发商 OnePWR 两年前订购、却无力支付的。含安装大约每 kW 2000 美元，而它买到的是队列中的位置。

这些电力对他们值多少：
—— 一个 72 GPU 的 GB300 机架峰值 155kW，加上冷却按每 GPU 2.5kW 算。1.7GW 大约可运行 68 万颗
—— 按五年合同下 GB300 约每小时 4 美元的价格，这相当于每月约 20 亿美元的算力
—— 所以这些发电机的成本大约相当于它们所启动的算力七周的收入

（我还不知道它们在哪里运行，也不知道它们是主供电还是电网接入……（全文见下方分作者部分）

为何重要：Anthropic 34 亿美元买 1.7GW 发电机：电力排队位置的稀缺价值，对现场发电与电力设备链条有直接含义。

#7 @jukan05 · 2026-11-09 09:38 北京时间

要点：台湾半导体分析师陆行之（Andrew Lu）对黄仁勋近期丑闻的看法：

美国为何借丑闻出鞘：三大战略目标

这起丑闻的目的绝不是揭露一段婚外情，而是要强行推进三个此前因黄仁勋反对而在中美科技战中毫无进展的关键目标。

第一刀：彻底摧毁黄仁勋在华盛顿游说反对制裁的正当性

过去两年，硅谷反对全面禁止对华芯片出口声音最大、影响力最强的企业领袖就是黄仁勋。他一直对白宫说同样的话：“把中国市场让给华为，最后死掉的只会是美国科技产业。”

但一旦美国情报部门把与黄仁勋有染的女子定性为“为中国国家安全部工作、试图引导美国放松芯片管制的间谍”，他为放松管制所做的每一次游说都带上了完全不同的政治含义。这变成了一个国家安全问题，引发外国间谍利用秘密关系施加影响的可能性。

黄仁勋再也无法在国会或白宫面前为中国市场说一句话。如果他……（全文见下方分作者部分）

为何重要：陆行之的“三大战略目标”框架，解释了事件可能如何被用于推进出口管制与境外租用限制。

#8 ★@dnystedt · 2026-11-08 22:40 北京时间

要点：媒体报道称，ASML 正在扮演 DRAM 造王者的角色：三星和 SK 海力士争夺 ASML 的 EUV 光刻机——扩大先进 DRAM 产能的关键。报道补充说，这两家韩国巨头各自投资 10 万亿至 20 万亿韩元（72 亿至 144 亿美元）购买 EUV 设备，计划 2027–2028 年交付，但由于需求旺盛，交付时间延长——最长可达以往最长时间的两倍，他们可能不得不等待。

ASML 在第三季度电话会上表示，当前 2027–2028 年的供应计划无法满足需求。三星和 SK 海力士将从明年开始分阶段接收 EUV 设备。能够确保按时交付的一方将领先另一方一步。

今年 3 月，SK 海力士披露了一笔 11.95 万亿韩元的 EUV 设备订单，交付期限为 2027 年底。$ASML $SKHY $SSNLF #Sam……（全文见下方分作者部分）

为何重要：ASML EUV 交期翻倍、2027–28 供应不足：DRAM 扩产的硬约束，支撑存储供给偏紧的多头逻辑。

$NBIS

Nebius 的新合同用 1.8 到 2.8 年的收入就能收回整个建设成本，对于本应使用六年的硬件来说，这似乎很短。

一兆瓦的 GB300 容量大约是 7.4 个机架加上建筑和电力。每个机架 370 万至 650 万美元，数据中心每兆瓦 1310 万至 1540 万美元，建设成本为每兆瓦 4050 万至 6350 万美元。对照来看：

1. 按每兆瓦每年 1200 万美元（Nebius 的 2026 年基准），需要 3.4 到 5.3 年。
2. 按 2000 万美元（其新核心合同的下限），需要 2 到 3.2 年。
3. 按 2250 万美元（2000 万至 2500 万美元区间的中值），需要 1.8 到 2.8 年。
4. 按 4500 万美元（大致是其短期定价），需要 0.9 到 1.4 年。

这是扣除电力、托管、人员、税收和利息之前的收入，所以并不是现金回收期。尽管如此，一个预期有六年盈利的卖方，本可以按更长的回收期定价，并借此赢得更多量。

两到三年的回收期符合这几类卖方：预期资产价值很快就会消失的；拥有自知不会持久的短缺所带来的定价权的；或者只是想在搞清楚之前先把钱拿回来的。

不过，仅凭回收期无法告诉我们 Nebius 相信的是哪一种，我的猜测是它按合同期限来定价。

原文：$NBIS

nebius's new contracts pay back the whole build in 1.8 to 2.8 years of revenue, which seems short for hardware that's supposed to last six.

a megawatt of GB300 capacity is about 7.4 racks plus the building and power. with a rack at $3.7m to $6.5m and the data centre at $13.1m to $15.4m a megawatt, the build costs $40.5m to $63.5m a megawatt. against that:

1. at $12m a megawatt a year, nebius's 2026 base, it takes 3.4 to 5.3 years.
2. at $20m, the bottom of its new core contracts, 2 to 3.2 years.
3. at $22.5m, the middle of the $20m to $25m range, 1.8 to 2.8 years.
4. at $45m, roughly its short-term pricing, 0.9 to 1.4 years.

that's revenue before power, colocation, staff, tax and interest, so it isn't cash payback. still, a seller that expected six years of earnings could price for a longer payback and win more volume with it.

a payback of two to three years fits a seller who expects the asset's value to be gone soon after, one with the pricing power of a shortage it knows won't last, or one that just wants its money back before it finds out.

the payback alone wont tell us which of those nebius believes, though my guess is it's pricing for the length of the contract.引用原帖（@nodiligence）：Every time I check how long a GPU lasts, the answer depends on who says what. Nvidia says eight years or more, neoclouds book six and their banks underwrite three to four... so I stopped asking which is right &amp; started wondering who pays when it is wrong. https://t.co/fzXJeEphKq 链接

2026-11-09 18:05 北京时间 ｜ Source: @nodiligence ｜ 原帖链接

对于一家 AI 实验室来说，燃气发电机与它所启动的芯片相比如此便宜，以至于它愿意付出任何代价尽早得到一台。

Anthropic 花 34 亿美元买下了 1.7GW 的 Caterpillar 发电机，这些是圣安东尼奥的电力开发商 OnePWR 两年前订购、却无力支付的。含安装大约每 kW 2000 美元，而它买到的是队列中的位置。

这些电力对他们值多少：
—— 一个 72 GPU 的 GB300 机架峰值 155kW，加上冷却按每 GPU 2.5kW 算。1.7GW 大约可运行 68 万颗
—— 按五年合同下 GB300 约每小时 4 美元的价格，这相当于每月约 20 亿美元的算力
—— 所以这些发电机的成本大约相当于它们所启动的算力七周的收入

（我还不知道它们在哪里运行，也不知道它们是主供电还是电网接入后的备用电源。如果是备用，它们能让一个站点在公用事业线路准备好之前数月就开机，而这些月份每个月同样值 20 亿美元。）

所以队列价格只会朝一个方向走。一家有每月 20 亿美元理由插队的实验室，会出价高过任何必须以同一台发电机作抵押借款的 neocloud，因为 neocloud 的贷款方是按燃气电厂而不是按七周 GPU 租金来估值它的。

Caterpillar 要多久才会开始根据 GPU 租金而不是制造成本来给发电机定价？

原文：for an AI lab, a gas generator is so cheap next to the chips it switches on that it'll pay whatever it takes to get one early.

anthropic paid $3.4bn for 1.7GW of caterpillar generators that onepwr, a san antonio power developer, ordered two years ago and couldn't fund. that's about $2,000 per kW with the install, and what it bought was the place in the queue.

what that power is worth to them:
-- a 72 GPU GB300 rack peaks at 155kW, so with cooling call it 2.5kW a GPU. 1.7GW runs about 680,000 of them
-- at the ~$4 an hour a GB300 goes for on a five year contract, that's about $2bn of compute a month
-- so the generators cost about seven weeks of what they switch on

(i don't know yet where they run or whether they're the main supply or backup behind a grid hookup. if it's backup, they're what lets a site switch on months before the utility's line is ready, and those months are worth the same $2bn each.)

so the queue price only goes one way. a lab with a $2bn a month reason to skip the line will outbid any neocloud that has to borrow against the same generator, because the neocloud's lender values it like a gas plant, not like seven weeks of GPU rent.

how long before caterpillar starts pricing its generators off the GPU rent instead of what they cost to build?

$AVGO $AMD $MSFT $ORCL $SPCX $CRWV $META $GOOGL

民主党拿下国会两天后，SpaceX 和 CoreWeave 的 CDS 创下纪录，而 Bloomberg 报道称大型超大规模云厂商的保护成本实际上变便宜了。所以市场已经在把为 AI 建设买单的公司与为其提供融资的公司区分开来。

看看现在谁在持有风险，这就说得通了。

—— Broadcom 为 Anthropic 的芯片融资提供了约 720 亿美元的担保，
—— AMD 对合作伙伴数据中心的担保在一个季度内从 41 亿美元升至 77 亿美元。

路透引用 UBS 的估计称，美国计划于 2028 年投产的数据中心容量中约 30% 可能被暂停。

如果发生这种情况，我认为 Microsoft 或 Meta 只是少花点钱，但为租约提供担保或以建设项目为抵押借款的一方仍然要承担责任，而且 12 月加息概率回到 75% 以上，这些钱短期内不会变便宜。

从谁还能借到钱也能看出来。
—— Meta 上周发行了 150 亿美元美元债和 90 亿欧元欧元债，抛售当天欧元债仍高于面值，而
—— Oracle 完成了 200 亿美元发行，但其债券比 2 月时多付了最多 175 个基点。

奇怪的是，设定这些价格的市场有多小。IG 指数中十个 AI 名字的净 CDS 约为 290 亿美元，对应约 8000 亿美元债务，所以我认为几家银行和基金同时对冲就能把它推动很多，我一直在想，这些纪录中有多少是真正的恐惧，有多少只是大家同时在对冲。

原文：$AVGO $AMD $MSFT $ORCL $SPCX $CRWV $META $GOOGL

two days after democrats took congress, spacex and coreweave CDS hit records, and bloomberg reported that protection on the big hyperscalers actually got cheaper. so the market is already pulling apart the companies paying for the AI build from the ones that financed it.

which kind of makes sense when you look at who is holding the risk now.

-- broadcom has about $72bn of backstops on anthropic's chip financing,
-- AMD's guarantees on partner data centres went from $4.1bn to $7.7bn in one quarter.

reuters quoted a UBS estimate that about 30% of the US data centre capacity planned for 2028 could get paused.

if that happens, i think microsoft or meta just spend less, but whoever guaranteed the leases or borrowed against the build is still on the hook, and with december hike odds back above 75% that money isn't getting cheaper any time soon.

you can see it in who can still borrow too.
-- meta sold $15bn of dollar bonds and €9bn of euro bonds last week and the euro bonds were above par on the day of the sell-off, while
-- oracle got its $20bn done but paid up to 175bp more on its bonds than it did in february.

the strange part is how small the market setting these prices is. there's about $29bn of net CDS on the ten AI names in the IG index against roughly $800bn of debt, so i think a few banks and funds hedging at the same time can push it around a lot, and i keep wondering how much of these records is real fear and how much is just everyone hedging at once.

2026-11-09 08:41 北京时间 ｜ Source: @GavinSBaker ｜ 原帖链接

Nvidia 董事会今天表示，没有理由相信 Jensen 的行为导致国家安全或 Nvidia 机密遭到泄露。

他们正在启动一项由独立董事组成的委员会监督、并由外部独立法律顾问协助的独立调查，将审查所有可获得的信息。鉴于这些独立董事负有的受托责任，这将是一项公正、详尽而彻底的调查。

Jensen 表示：“我支持这项调查，并将全力配合。我热爱我们的国家和我们的公司。我致力于保护 NVIDIA 并支持美国的国家安全。我为身为美国人感到自豪和感激。”

我认识 Jensen 将近 30 年了。我根本不相信他会故意做任何对美国或 Nvidia 不利的事情。

他对我谈到中国时唯一说过的，就是保持以 Nvidia 为首的美国硬件生态领先于中国的绝对重要性。仅此而已。

更详细的想法见下文：

原文：Nvidia’s board stated today that it has found “no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised” as a result of Jensen’s actions.

They are initiating an independent investigation overseen by a committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, that will examine all available information. This will be an unbiased, exhaustive and thorough investigation given the fiduciary responsibilities of these independent directors.

Jensen said that “I support the investigation and will cooperate fully. I love our country and our company. I am committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America’s national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American.”

I have known Jensen for almost 30 years. I simply do not believe he would ever knowingly do anything to disadvantage either America or Nvidia.

The only thing he has ever said to me about China is the absolute importance of keeping the American hardware ecosystem, led by Nvidia, ahead of China. That’s it.

More detailed thoughts follow below:

2026-11-09 08:41 北京时间 ｜ Source: @GavinSBaker ｜ 原帖链接

Jensen 曾说，在 Nvidia GPU 方面，美国应该拥有“最先、最多、最好”的。在这些 GPU 首次在美国上市很久之后，他一直主张只向中国提供性能较弱的版本。

在我看来，这似乎是美国最好的国防政策，因为它在保持美国领先中国的同时，降低了华为发展出有竞争力的 AI 硬件生态的几率。允许与中国军方无关的中国 AI 实验室在东南亚使用云服务，与这一思路是一致的。如果与中国爆发公开敌对，这一切都可以瞬间关闭，这将使美国在任何冲突中拥有显著优势。

按定义，如果中国拥有一个强大的、由华为主导的替代性 AI 硬件生态，我们就不会有这种优势。而完全限制中国获取美国硬件，最终将导致一个不同生态的形成——鉴于中国电力过剩，这个生态不会针对能效进行优化。

中国正在开发大型光学 scale-up 域，这需要中国大量拥有的激光衬底和电力。这些光学 scale-up 域可以在系统层面抵消单颗美国 GPU 的大部分优势，代价是消耗多得多的电力。华为 Atlas 960e 拥有 4096 颗芯片的光学 scale-up 域，而 NVL72 只有 72 颗 GPU 的 scale-up 域。向中国提供 1）有限的、劣于美国可得版本的 GPU，以及 2）在友好亚洲国家使用 GPU 云服务的渠道，能够阻滞中国的这一努力，因为这削弱了中国实验室投资于利用这种截然不同系统架构所需的软件和替代模型架构的动力。

理性的人可以对此有不同看法，但需要注意的是，拜登和特朗普两届政府中都有官员支持这一政策，同样两届政府中也都有官员反对。假装这是个简单问题对所有人都没有好处，这也是为什么 Jensen 所倡导的政策既有两党支持也有两党反对。

而这一切正是中国限制获取 Nvidia GPU 的原因。中国理解以上所有内容，并希望发展自己的、在冲突中无法被关掉的替代 AI 生态。中国实验室经常向投资者抱怨自己“GPU 贫穷”，并为自己在本国政府实际上束缚其手脚的情况下仍具竞争力而自豪。我不确定中国希望从这次间谍行动中得到什么，毕竟他们在限制 Nvidia GPU 在中国境内的销售，但值得考虑的是，让 Jensen 被撤换 Nvidia CEO 一职，可能正符合中国自身的国家安全目标。

Jensen 对他的家人和同事表达了深深的歉意，并对自己的行为承担全部责任。我认为在董事会调查完成之前暂缓进一步判断是明智的。

就个人层面而言，Jensen 多年来一直是我的朋友。在我认识他的时间里，他始终是爱国且深深亲美的。他*总是*说，他的故事只有在美国才可能发生。

原文：Jensen has said that America should have “the first, the most and the best” when it comes to Nvidia’s GPUs. He has consistently advocated for giving China less powerful versions of those GPUs long after they were first available in America.

From my perspective, this seems like the best national defense policy for America as it lowers the odds of Huawei developing a competitive AI hardware ecosystem while keeping America ahead of China. Letting Chinese AI labs that are not affiliated with the Chinese military use cloud services in Southeast Asia is consonant with this. All of this can be turned off in an instant in the event of outright hostilities with China which would give America a significant advantage in any conflict.

Definitionally, we would not have this advantage if China had a robust, Huawei led alternative AI hardware ecosystem. And completely restricting China’s access to American hardware would eventually result in the development of a different ecosystem that was not optimized for power efficiency given China’s surfeit of power.

China is working on developing large optical scale-up domains, which require the laser substrates and power that China has in abundance. These optical scale-up domains can offset much of the superiority of individual American GPUs at a system level at the cost of consuming dramatically more power. The Huawei Atlas 960e has a 4096 chip optical scale-up domains vs. the 72 GPU scale-up domain in the NVL72. Giving China 1) limited access to GPUs inferior to those available in America and 2) China access to GPU cloud services in friendly Asian countries stalls this Chinese effort by disincentivizing Chinese labs from investing in the software and alternative model architectures required to exploit this very different system architecture.

Reasonable minds can disagree about this, but important to note that officials in both the Biden and Trump administrations supported this policy and likewise officials in both administrations opposed it. Pretending that it is a simple question does everyone a disservice, which is why there is both bipartisan support and bipartisan opposition to the policy that Jensen has advocated.

And all of this is why China restricts access to Nvidia GPUs. China understands all of the above and wants to develop its own alternative AI ecosystem that cannot be turned off in the event of a conflict. Chinese labs regularly complain to investors about being “GPU poor” and take pride in the fact that they are competitive despite their own government effectively tying their hands. I’m not sure what China hoped to gain from this espionage operation given they are restricting sales of Nvidia GPUs inside of China, but it is worth considering that having Jensen removed as the CEO of Nvidia might serve China’s own national security goals.

Jensen expressed deep regret to his family and his colleagues for his actions while taking full responsibility for them. I think it is wise to defer further judgment until the board’s investigation has been completed.

On a personal level, Jensen has been a friend of mine for many long years. I have never known him to be anything other than patriotic and deeply pro-American. He *always* says that his story would have only been possible in America.所回复原帖（@GavinSBaker）：Nvidia’s board stated today that it has found “no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised” as a result of Jensen’s actions.

They are initiating an independent investigation overseen by a committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, that will examine all available information. This will be an unbiased, exhaustive and thorou… 链接

2026-11-09 09:29 北京时间 ｜ Source: @GavinSBaker ｜ 原帖链接

对的部分：“这导致了一种奇怪的局面：两国政府中不同的官员群体同时把向中国出售 H200 视为一种需要争取和/或需要防范的‘让步’。”

完全错误的部分：“尽管中国可能并非有意，但它意外地实现了两全其美：一个免受美国出口竞争的受保护硬件市场，以及一个被悄悄赋能、可以租用或走私其追赶美国前沿所需任何资源的软件产业。”

如果中国软件产业（即 AI 实验室）“被悄悄赋能、可以租用或走私其追赶美国前沿所需任何资源”，那么这个“受保护的硬件市场”在功能上就是无用的。

如果实验室不积极使用，你就无法开发出有竞争力的硬件。投资者在评估美国本土的加速器初创公司时，把这称为“冷启动”问题。如果实验室不用，AI 硬件就无法演进、无法具备竞争力。

这就是中国限制获取 Nvidia GPU 的原因。

原文：Yes: “This has led to a bizarre situation where different sets of officials across both governments simultaneously view the sale of H200s to China as a “concession” to be fought for and/or guarded against.:”

Utterly wrong: “Though it probably did not mean to, China has managed to accidentally achieve the best of both worlds: a captive hardware market protected from competitive U.S. exports, and a software industry quietly empowered to rent or smuggle whatever resources it needs to chase the American frontier.”

The “captive hardware market” is functionally useless if the Chinese software industry, i.e. AI labs, are “quietly empowered to rent or smuggle whatever resources it needs to chase the American frontier.”

You cannot develop competitive hardware without the labs actively using it. Investors call this the “cold start” problem when evaluating accelerator startups here in America. AI hardware cannot evolve and become competitive if labs don’t use it.

That is why China restricts access to Nvidia GPUs.所回复原帖（@RyanFedasiuk）：@GavinSBaker This is the reason:

随着模型改进，越来越多地，模型*就是*产品。

留给产品要做的只剩下：
- 加入足够的结构来暴露模型能力，同时不束缚它们
- 不要加太多结构，否则下个月模型就会把产品吃掉

原文：As the models improve, increasingly the models *are* the product

All that’s left for product to do is:
- add enough structure to expose model capabilities w/out constraining them
- don’t add too much structure that the model will eat the product next month

@evergreencap3（保留 1 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 1 条）

2026-11-09 02:06 北京时间 ｜ Source: @evergreencap3 ｜ 原帖链接

同意。这取决于价格为什么下跌。

如果更好的算法降低了某项工作负载的成本、并且节省被传导下去，那么通过 Jevons 效应所有人都赢。

但如果是因为客户不愿意支付更高价格，那就只是降价，供应商每个 token 赚取的利润率更低（尽管销量仍可能增长到足以在总量上产生更多利润）。

人们每次都拿 Jevons 说事，但现实是 token 价格下跌这两个原因都有。

不过至少对终端客户而言，更好的模型能力加上更低的价格，在任何一种情况下都会带来显著更高的 ROI，这一直在推动 token 用量持续拐点式增长，在我看来对整个生态是积极的。

原文：Agreed. It depends on why price dropped.

If a better algorithm cuts the cost of a workload and the savings are passed through, everyone wins via Jevons.

But if it's because customers are unwilling to pay higher prices, that's just a price cut and the provider earns lower margin per token (though volumes can still increase enough to produce more profit dollars in aggregate).

People point to Jevons every time but the reality is token prices are falling for both reasons.

At least for end-customers, though, better model capabilities combined with falling prices result in meaningfully higher ROI in either instance, which has been driving the ongoing inflection in token volumes, and imo is positive for the broader ecosystem.所回复原帖（@bernhardsson）：Feels like a lot of Jevon’s paradox arguments are a bit sloppy.

Prices go up: “great for revenue”
Prices go down: “Jevon’s paradox, great for revenue”

Like, you should be honest about the sign of the partial derivative of revenue wrt prices 链接

@flyingwhale919（保留 1 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 1 条）

2026-11-09 19:31 北京时间 ｜ Source: @flyingwhale919 ｜ 原帖链接

美股AI基建板块在上周经历了灾难性的一周，许多人归咎于民主党中选胜出带来的冲击，并担心AI数据中心建设狂潮就此转向。我对此倒不担心，详见：https://t.co/G31ESTBP5A

—美国中选容易让人产生一种错觉：共和党支持AI基建，民主党反对AI基建。因此中选结果出来，民主党大胜必然意味着AI基建的衰落。但事情没有那么简单，实际情况是：越来越多的选民反对AI基建，因此共和党也不得不调整了策略，也开始强调加强AI监管。因此，我认为AI基建本身就面临阶段性逆风(AI监管审批的加强，是两党共识），只是美国中选成为了一个引爆点。

—在最新选举结果出来后，虽然民主党仍然有议员提出永久禁止AI的提案，但是更多胜选的民主党候任州长已经展现出灵活务实的一面，表态说要在保护民众的利益这个前提下，继续推进AI建设（美国版的“两手抓，两手都要硬”）。

—已经有几个关键的AI基础设施厂商表态：1）加强与社区民众的沟通 2）承担更多，包括强调企业承担自身电力成本、帮助降低居民电费、增加教师和公共服务人员收益、培训和雇用当地劳动力等。3）考虑多地（包括海外布局）。从这些表态可以看出，厂商这一块会付出更多，包括时间，精力，与金钱，但并没有退缩。

$NVDA $AMD $META $CRWV $MSFT $GOOG $AMZN $NBIS

—对AI基建潮流后继发展的判断：

从产业的角度，AI基建仍然会增长（最核心的原因在于终端需求仍然在增长），但增长速度会放缓（建设过程中面对的困难在增加，原来可能仅是工程这个角度，现在则至少要增加民众支持，监管，人力，财力等多个维度）

从股市的角度，则AI基建板块中期面临的最大问题是估值的下调。股价是由基本面和估值两大因素共同决定的。当基本面向好时，人们很容易给予更高的估值，形成“双击”；但是当基本面遭遇短期挫折的时候，不确定因素的增加，会导致估值同步下挫，形成“双杀”。但是，通常人们的焦点在于基本面，而常常忽略了估值的变化。

【AI infra/半导体】

@jukan05（保留 14 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 25 条）

2026-11-09 09:38 北京时间 ｜ Source: @jukan05 ｜ 原帖链接

台湾半导体分析师陆行之（Andrew Lu）对黄仁勋近期丑闻的看法：

美国为何借丑闻出鞘：三大战略目标

这起丑闻的目的绝不是揭露一段婚外情，而是要强行推进三个此前因黄仁勋反对而在中美科技战中毫无进展的关键目标。

第一刀：彻底摧毁黄仁勋在华盛顿游说反对制裁的正当性

过去两年，硅谷反对全面禁止对华芯片出口声音最大、影响力最强的企业领袖就是黄仁勋。他一直对白宫说同样的话：“把中国市场让给华为，最后死掉的只会是美国科技产业。”

但一旦美国情报部门把与黄仁勋有染的女子定性为“为中国国家安全部工作、试图引导美国放松芯片管制的间谍”，他为放松管制所做的每一次游说都带上了完全不同的政治含义。这变成了一个国家安全问题，引发外国间谍利用秘密关系施加影响的可能性。

黄仁勋再也无法在国会或白宫面前为中国市场说一句话。如果他这么做，就会被打上“落入情报行动却仍执迷不悟”的标签。

第二刀：撤销对华 H200 出口批准，切断中国空壳公司与东南亚走私网络之间的联系

看看两篇报道的时间点。

11 月 8 日，也就是黄仁勋丑闻曝光的同一天，《纽约时报》发表了一篇调查报道，记者亲自走访了美国商务部批准经手 Nvidia H200 芯片的数十家中国公司，北京的 New Matrix 就是其中之一。报道称，获准购买芯片的公司注册地址原来是空荡荡的“幽灵办公室”，部分公司与美国制裁名单上的实体及与中国军方有合同关系的企业关系密切。

一篇爆料说 Nvidia CEO 与经手芯片走私的新加坡公司 Megaspeed 的一名女间谍有染；另一篇说商务部批准购买 H200 的中国公司不过是代中国军方交易的空壳公司。这两篇报道的目的很明确：向特朗普政府和商务部施压，要求全面撤销或冻结对华 H200 出口批准，并进一步强制全面修补让芯片经由新加坡及东南亚其他地方第三方运营数据中心转移的漏洞。

第三刀：通过国会监督和独立调查给 Nvidia 套上国家安全的缰绳

作为全球市值最大的公司和 AI 领域的主导力量，Nvidia 此前在业务经营上享有广泛的自主权。但这一事件之后，负责中国、外交和情报事务的三个国会委员会已开始直接审查 Nvidia 的内部安全措施。它们甚至迫使 Nvidia 董事会成立独立调查委员会，调查公司自己的创始人兼 CEO。

实际上，华盛顿的国家安全体系已在 Nvidia 董事会内部安插了一只看门狗。从现在起，目标是确保一切——直到 Nvidia 每一颗高性能芯片的去向——都严格符合美国国家安全战略。这也意味着再也没有游走在规则边缘、或发布专门针对监管门槛定制规格芯片的空间。

结论

“丑闻是一枚准时引爆的雷管。真正的目标是堵住芯片外流的漏洞。”

黄仁勋犯了在私生活和个人关系上放松警惕的致命错误。最终他落入了情报战的教科书式手段——美人计，并被策反。美国国家安全和情报机构无情地利用了他私生活暴露出的弱点。结果，他们一举解决了三个棘手问题：Nvidia 在白宫游说放松对华限制、H200 芯片流入中国空壳公司，以及经由新加坡的走私。

定于 11 月 18 日举行的国会闭门听证会，将是美国认真着手进一步收紧对中国 AI 算力制裁的决定性转折点。

原文：Taiwanese semiconductor analyst Andrew Lu’s take on the recent scandal involving Jensen Huang:

Why the US Seized on the Scandal to Draw Its Sword: Three Strategic Objectives

The purpose of this scandal is by no means to expose an affair. It is to force through three critical objectives that had made no headway in the tech war between the US and China because Jensen Huang opposed them.

The first cut: Completely destroy the legitimacy of Jensen Huang's lobbying against sanctions in Washington

Over the past two years, the loudest and most influential business leader in Silicon Valley opposing a total ban on chip exports to China has been Jensen Huang. He has told the White House the same thing all along: "Cede the Chinese market to Huawei, and the only one that ends up dead is the American tech industry."

But the moment US intelligence labels the woman named as Huang's affair partner "a spy working for China's Ministry of State Security who sought to steer the US toward easing its chip controls," every bit of lobbying he has done for looser controls takes on an entirely different political meaning. It becomes a national security matter, raising the possibility that a foreign spy used a secret relationship to exert influence.

Huang can no longer say a single word in defense of the Chinese market before Congress or the White House. If he does, he will be branded as someone who "fell for an intelligence operation and still hasn't come to his senses."

The second cut: Reverse the H200 export approvals for China and sever the link between Chinese shell companies and Southeast Asian smuggling networks

Look at the timing of the two reports.

On November 8, the very day the Jensen Huang scandal broke, The New York Times published an investigation for which its reporters went in person to dozens of Chinese companies that the US Commerce Department had approved to handle Nvidia's H200 chips. New Matrix in Beijing was one of them. According to the report, the registered addresses of companies approved to buy the chips turned out to be empty "ghost offices," and some of the companies had close ties to entities on US sanctions lists and to firms under contract with the Chinese military.

One exposé says Nvidia's CEO was having an affair with a female spy from Megaspeed, the Singapore company that handled the chip smuggling. The other says the Chinese companies Commerce approved to buy H200s are nothing but shell companies trading on behalf of the Chinese military. The aim of these two reports is clear. They are meant to pressure the Trump administration and the Commerce Department into fully revoking or freezing H200 export approvals for China and, going further, to force a sweeping overhaul of the loophole that let chips be diverted through data centers run by third parties in Singapore and elsewhere in Southeast Asia.

The third cut: Put Nvidia on a national security leash through congressional oversight and an independent investigation

As the world's largest company by market cap and the dominant force in AI, Nvidia has until now enjoyed broad autonomy in how it runs its business. But in the wake of this incident, the three congressional committees responsible for China, foreign affairs, and intelligence have begun examining Nvidia's internal security measures directly. They have even pressured Nvidia's board into forming an independent investigation committee to look into the company's own founder and CEO.

In effect, Washington's national security establishment has planted a watchdog right inside Nvidia's board. From now on, the goal is to make sure that everything, down to where each and every one of Nvidia's high performance chips ends up, conforms strictly to US national security strategy. It also means there is no more room for skirting the edge of the rules or releasing chips with specs specially tailored to regulatory thresholds.

Conclusion

"The scandal was a detonator set off right on cue. The real goal was to plug the holes chips were leaking through."

Jensen Huang made the fatal mistake of letting his guard down in his private life and personal relationships. In the end, he fell into a honey trap, the textbook tactic of intelligence warfare, and was compromised. America's national security and intelligence agencies ruthlessly exploited the weakness his private life had exposed. As a result, they solved three thorny problems in a single stroke: Nvidia's lobbying at the White House to ease restrictions on China, the flow of H200 chips into Chinese shell companies, and smuggling routed through Singapore.

The closed congressional hearing set for November 18 will be the decisive turning point at which the US moves in earnest to tighten its sanctions on China's AI compute even further.

2026-11-09 14:58 北京时间 ｜ Source: @jukan05 ｜ 原帖链接

（韩文报道翻译）9 日确认，三星电子半导体蚀刻工艺配方已泄露给美国设备商 Lam Research 的韩国业务组织。

Lam Research 向三星电子半导体生产线供应蚀刻和沉积等设备。设备运行中产生的数据存储在生产线上安装的监控服务器等处。

Lam Research 为改进设备性能和维护，可在与三星电子约定的范围内接收部分数据。韩国组织 Lam Research Korea 向三星电子申请并接收这些数据。

在此过程中，三星电子未允许外传的工艺配方信息以单独文件形式流向了 Lam Research Korea。三星电子于今年下半年察觉此事。调查结果显示，由于 Lam Research Korea 一方的不当请求，超出许可范围的信息外传曾多次重复发生。

原文：삼성전자 반도체 식각 공정 레시피가 미국 장비업체 램리서치의 한국 사업 조직에 유출된 것으로 9일 확인됐다.

램리서치는 삼성전자 반도체 생산라인에 식각과 증착 장비 등을 공급한다. 장비를 운용하면서 발생하는 데이터는 생산라인에 설치된 모니터링 서버 등에 저장된다.

램리서치는 장비 성능 개선과 유지보수 등을 위해 삼성전자와 합의한 범위 안에서 일부 데이터를 받을 수 있었다. 한국 조직인 램리서치코리아가 삼성전자에 요청해 이 데이터를 받아 왔다.

이 과정에서 삼성전자가 반출을 허용하지 않은 공정 레시피 정보가 별도 파일로 램리서치코리아에 넘어간 것으로 확인됐다. 삼성전자는 올해 하반기 이 사안을 인지했다. 조사를 벌인 결과 램리서치코리아 측 부정 요청으로 허용 범위를 벗어난 정보 반출이 수차례 반복됐던 것으로 전해졌다.所回复原帖（@GrollPaw）：@jukan05 뭘 유출한건가요? 链接

2026-11-08 23:52 北京时间 ｜ Source: @aleabitoreddit ｜ 原帖链接

本周 AI 抛售期间，从 Qualcomm、Arm 到 Meta 高管的各种评论：

$LITE CEO：“IREN 的 CEO 说过一句话，我也赞同。我们在需求订单簿上看到的，与市场所表现出的之间存在巨大脱节。而你却看到了大规模、大规模的抛售。”（11 月 6 日周五）

$AMD CEO 接受 CNBC 采访：“我们正处于采用周期的最初阶段，人们能够看到 AI 和超级智能将带我们走向何方。”

“我们看到需求高于供给。”（11 月 4 日周三）

$ARM CEO 谈 AI 基础设施支出是否即将见顶的争论：

“我认为在一些变得更难的州，其他州可能会接手、让它变得更容易，但我坚信，如果美国在数据中心建设方面以任何方式犹豫……

“世界其他地区已准备好并愿意。对这种容量的需求巨大。我认为在全球范围内，美国确实希望能够与中国竞争。中国一点也没有放慢。”（11 月 5 日周四）

$APLD CEO：银行建设贷款、项目融资和债券市场仍然可用，尽管利率和信用利差有所上升

“最大的风险是按时交付。”“需求状况极其强劲。”（11 月 2 日周一）

$QCOM CEO：“不是需求受限，而是供给受限。”

“我们缺晶圆、缺基板、缺封装。一切都短缺，因为需求非常、非常高。”

“我认为市场在反应的是算力建设的速度。”

$META 谈政治风险：“昨晚确实有一位把数据中心作为其竞选纲领重要部分的州长胜选。

他说一过就职典礼我们就见面吧，因为我们不想在我们镇上失去这笔投资。我们不想让这些工作机会流向别处。”（11 月 4 日周三）

即使是曾竞选反对数据中心的政客也在谈判。但 Meta 总裁也强调，在美国新建数据中心将变得更加困难。

总结：
-> 加速器厂商、网络和半导体公司的评论极其乐观
-> 数据中心开发商报告定价/需求强劲，但越来越强调融资、许可和执行风险（更谨慎）
-> 此前竞选反对数据中心的政客正表现出合作态度

需求消失与需求因无法足够快地建设而被推迟，二者之间有重要区别。

尽管抛售很严重，但我在这些评论中没有看到广泛的 AI 需求毁灭的证据。

原文：Various commentary from Qualcomm, Arm, to Meta executives this week during the AI selloff:

$LITE CEO: "You had a comment from the CEO of $IREN and I'd second that. There's a massive disconnect in what we're seeing in our demand books.

And what the markets are showing. And yet you're seeing a massive, massive selloff." (Fri, Nov 6)

$AMD CEO CNBC: "We're at the very beginning of the adoption cycle, where people can see where AI and superintelligence will take us"

"We are seeing demand being higher than supply" (Wed, Nov 4)

$ARM CEO regarding a debate whether AI infrastructure spending is about to peak:

"I think in some states where it gets harder, other states may pick it up to make it easier, but I do believe strongly that if the US hesitates in any way shape performance in terms of data center buildout...

"The rest of the world is ready and willing. There is just huge demand for this capacity. And I think globally the US really wants to be able to compete against China. China is not slowing down at all." (Thu, Nov 5)

$APLD CEO: Bank construction lending, project financing and bond markets remain available, although interest rates and credit spreads have risen

"The big risk is delivering on time." "The demand picture is extremely robust." (Mon, Nov 2)

$QCOM CEO: "It's not demand bound. It's a supply bound."

"We're short on wafers, we're short on substrate, we're short on assembly. Everything is short because demand is very, very high."

"I think what the market is reacting to is what is the speed of the compute build out."

$META on political risk: "We did have a governor that won last night who did make data centers a big part of the platform.

Say as soon as we get past the inauguration, let's meet because we don't want to lose this investment in our town. We don't want these jobs to go elsewhere." (Wed, Nov 4)

Even politicians campaigning against data centers are negotiating. But Meta's president also emphasized that starting new US DCs will become more difficult.

TLDR:
-> Extremely bullish commentary from accelerator players, networking, and semi companies
-> DC developers report strong pricing/demand, but increasingly emphasize financing, permitting and execution risks (more cautious)
-> Politicians that previously campaigned against DCs are showing cooperation

There's an important difference between demand disappearing and demand being delayed by the inability to build fast enough.

Despite the severity of the selloff, I'm not seeing evidence of widespread AI demand destruction in commentary.（video：仅预览图，完整内容见原帖）

★@tphuang（保留 7 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 11 条）

2026-11-09 04:18 北京时间 ｜ Source: ★@tphuang ｜ 原帖链接

有人问到这个报道。请记住，上海微电子（SMEE）的 SSA/SSB/SSC-800 光刻机是在中国努力与 ASML 竞争的同时开发的。

最初关于 i-Line 版本的报道并不好，但看起来他们已经追上了行业对大规模量产的预期，因为它已被逻辑和存储产线用于数千万片晶圆的生产，有效稼动率 >92%。

关键在于他们设计了更高产量的双工件台平台（ASML 所用的方案），并且关键部件 90% 以上采用国内供应商。所有真正关键的东西如镜头都使用国内供应商，以确保供应链可靠性。

请记住，干式 DUV 光刻机（即干式 SSA800）大约在同一时间进入验证。所以它应该处于差不多的状态。ArFi（浸润式）那款大约落后 2 年。

任何高科技设备的验证和采用都有一个漫长的过程。所以，不必花太多时间去想它。如果有外国出口管制，进程会加快；如果没有，进程会放慢。这里已经没有重大障碍了。

原文：Got asked abt this story. Keep in mind SMEE had SSA/SSB/SSC-800 lithography machines in development @ same time as China's effort to be competitive vs ASML.

Initial iLine variant report was not good, but looks like they have caught up to industry expectations for HVM, since it has already been used by logic & memory production line for 10s of millions of wafers w/ > 92% effective uptime.

Key here was that they designed the more higher volume dual-stage platform (what ASML uses) as well as utilizing 90%+ domestic suppliers for key components. All the really critical stuff like lens are using domestic suppliers to ensure supply chain reliability.

Keep in mind that dry DUV lithography machine (so dry SSA800) also went into validation at around the same time. So that one should be at about same status. Arfi one is ~2yrs behind.

There is a long process around validation & adoption of any kind of high tech equipment. So, don't spend too much time thinking about it. If there are foreign export control, the process will move faster. If there aren't, the process will move slower. There is no significant hurdles left here.（video：仅预览图，完整内容见原帖）

2026-11-08 21:39 北京时间 ｜ Source: ★@tphuang ｜ 原帖链接

中国开工建设第四代“炽龙一号”高温气冷堆（HTGR），作为徐圩大型核电站集群的一部分，配套 2 台华龙一号机组。

华龙一号产生的饱和蒸汽再加热至 541°C，供连云港石化集群使用。每年将生产 3250 万吨蒸汽 + 115 亿千瓦时电力。

这将是中国首个商业 HTGR 项目，总功率 600MW。它将生产石化裂解/重整装置所需的大量 460°C 蒸汽。

中国田湾核电站已在“和气一号”项目中为附近的石化设施供应工业热能，但只有 HTGR 能产生完全替代火电蒸汽所需的高温蒸汽。

鉴于中国石化集群位于同样建有核电站的沿海地区，这种脱碳蒸汽生产模式可以在全国复制。

原文：China started construction of 4th gen 炽龙一号 HTGR reactor as part of a large NPP cluster in 徐圩 w/ 2 Hualong-1 reactors.

Hualong-1 reactors produces saturated steam which gets reheated to 541C for 连云港 petrochem cluster. It will produce 32.5Mt steam + 11.5TWh power per yr.

This will be China's 1st commercial HTGR project w/ 600MW of total power. It will produce large qty of 460 C steam needed by petrochemical crackers/reformers.

China's Tianwan reactors already supply industrial heat for petrochem facility near by in 和气一号 project, but only HTGR can produce the high heat steam also needed to fully replace thermal steam.

Given that China's petrochem clusters are located in the coastal area where NPPs are also built, this de-carbonized steam generation can be replicated across China.

2026-11-09 00:29 北京时间 ｜ Source: @damnang2 ｜ 原帖链接

我想澄清我的观点，因为我认为我说过的一些话可能留下了被误解的空间。

1. 我的论点不是存储不再有周期性，而是这个行业正试图通过结构性变化超越其传统的周期模式。定制存储就是一个例子。

2. 这些努力是由 AI 对存储的爆炸性需求推动的。如果 AI 全是炒作、没有实质内容，那么这一切都不成立。

3. 三大存储公司明白自身业务的固有局限。这就是为什么他们试图利用这次 AI 机遇扩展业务。

4. 来自老牌芯片设计商的反弹也在增加。我认为 NVHBM 就是一个典型例子。

5. 我个人认为，如果存储公司未能在这轮 AI 繁荣中完成业务转型，行业很有可能回落到传统的周期模式。

这些是我反复表达过的观点。

我个人仍然认为我们处于早期阶段，需要观察事态如何发展。

原文：I want to clarify my views, as I think some of what I have said may have left room for misunderstanding.

1. My argument is not that memory is no longer cyclical. It is that the industry is trying to move beyond its traditional cyclical model through structural changes. Custom memory is one example.

2. These efforts are being driven by the explosive demand for memory from AI. If AI is all hype and has no real substance, none of this holds.

3. The three major memory companies understand the inherent limitations of their business. That is why they are trying to use this AI opportunity to expand their businesses.

4. Pushback from established chip designers is also growing. I see NVHBM as a prime example.

5. Personally, I believe that if memory companies fail to transform their businesses during this AI boom, there is a strong chance the industry will fall back into its traditional cyclical pattern.

These are the points I have made repeatedly.

Personally, I still think we are in the early stages, and we need to watch how this develops.引用原帖（@VKMacro）：If TSMC was still cyclical throughout years of a structural mobile phone market, why do we think memory has gone from max cyclical to structural?

The NVIDIA Board is initiating an independent investigation in light of recent reporting that founder and CEO Jensen Huang has been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

原文：🔔 The Daily Semi-Cap — November 8, 2026

📊 SMH: $463.96 (+3.62% vs November 5; last market close: November 6, 2026)

• Nvidia’s board launched an independent investigation into reports that Jensen Huang was targeted by a foreign intelligence effort. Independent directors and outside counsel will oversee it. The board expressed confidence in his leadership and said it has no reason to believe national security or company secrets were compromised. This is a governance overhang—not an announced leadership change.

• Anthropic reportedly agreed to pay $3.4B for 1.7GW of gas-fired generators, taking over OnePWR Solutions’ existing Caterpillar equipment orders and associated installation work. The transaction, struck in late September and newly disclosed today, is not an acquisition of OnePWR. Securing equipment slots can shorten the power bottleneck, but does not eliminate permitting, fuel-supply or construction requirements.

• Terafab’s equipment procurement team has approached Japanese semiconductor-tool manufacturers with ordering plans as Musk pushes to accelerate factory development. That moves the project beyond a capacity ambition toward supplier engagement. The distinction for equipment investors: a procurement roadmap is a potential demand signal, not booked revenue, installed capacity or proof that manufacturing yields will meet targets.

• Britain is resisting a blanket data-center slowdown. Growth minister Dan Tomlinson said construction should continue as essential infrastructure, with local investment and jobs required for government support. The existing roadmap anticipates at least 6GW of AI-capable capacity by 2030. The opportunity is geographic redistribution of infrastructure spending—not an escape from power constraints or community opposition.

• Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron are discussing common fab-robot charging standards with SEMI, including eventual integration of charging information with manufacturing execution systems. Shared infrastructure could make mixed robot fleets practical in older fabs where space is scarce and material handling remains manual. Standards and implementation responsibilities are still being developed; this is an automation-enablement initiative, not a disclosed equipment order.

【大人物】

★@levie（保留 2 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 2 条）

2026-11-09 02:50 北京时间 ｜ Source: ★@levie ｜ 原帖链接

AI 领域的一条好经验是：每隔 3 到 6 个月就应该回头看看最新进展，把以前做不到的事再试一遍，看看现在行不行。

在 AI 的历史上，没有哪个时期比现在更适用这一点。仅过去六个月，围绕计算机与工具使用、更少的 context rot、更好的推理、多模态，以及多个关键领域更深的专业知识，模型在可执行任务上的能力已经大幅提升。

这打开了 AI 系统可自主工作的新方向。基本上，任何有 API 的东西（甚至很多没有 API 的东西），我们都可以预期 agent 能够把它作为底层能力来控制和使用，以完成一个流程。

这意味着我们不再*仅仅*依赖模型从零生成一切，而是可以非常有效地使用现有系统（或为它们专门打造的新系统）。这为 agent 带来了巨大的杠杆。

安全 agent 可以利用现有数据源和网络安全工具，IT agent 可以在软件栈之间可靠地执行工作流，数据 agent 可以在系统之间迁移数据或流程，建筑与设计 agent 可以利用 CAD 和设计工具，等等。而今天任何还不好用的工具，很可能会被对 agent 友好的工具或开源工具取代，完成同样的事情。

当你把这一点与被训练进 agent 的领域理解结合起来，就会开始得到面向每一种岗位职能和每一个行业的自主协作者。如果你在构建时抱着这种认知，这将打开大量新的 AI 机会。

原文：A good lesson in AI is that you need to check back in on the latest with every 3 to 6 months to try something that wasn’t possible before and see if it works again.

At no point in AI’s history is that more true than today. Just the past six months of model progress around computer and tool use, less context rot, better reasoning, multimodality, and deeper domain expertise in a number of key fields, these models are vastly more capable on what they can execute.

This is opening up a new vector of what AI systems will be able to autonomously work on. Essentially, anything with an API (and even many things without), we can anticipate that an agent will be able to control and utilize as an underlying capability to complete a process.

This means that we are no longer dependent on *just* the models generating everything from whole cloth, but instead they can fully utilize existing systems (or new ones purpose built for them) very effectively. This creates immense leverage for agents.

Security agents can leverage existing data feeds and cyber tooling, IT agents that can execute workflows reliably across software stacks, data agents that can migrate data or processes between systems, architect and design agents can leverage CAD and design tools, and on and on. And any of today’s tools that don’t work will likely be replaced by agent-friendly or OSS tooling that does the same.

When you combine this with the domain understanding being trained into agents, you started to get autonomous collaborators for every job function and industry. This will open up a ton of new opportunities in AI if you build with this understanding in mind.

2026-11-09 08:44 北京时间 ｜ Source: ★@levie ｜ 原帖链接

我们正在从这样一个 AI 时代——输出大致以你与 AI 来回对话协作的速度产生——迈向另一个时期：你可以给一组 agent 布置一个问题，让它们去为你完成任意大量的工作。这意味着生产力提升可能从 20–50% 变成没有真正上限。

Agent 集群（swarm）以及把复杂工作分发给多个 agent 并行执行的能力，在许多领域带来巨大解锁。这将在我的领域带来性质上不同的结果。

这已经在编程领域显现。接下来，网络安全几乎完全是一个“更多 agent 审查更多东西就会带来更好结果”的问题空间。我们现在在许多科学领域也看到了同样的情况，这几乎肯定会改变生命科学。

而这在任何可以将基于计算机的工作并行化、以更快完成或达到完全不同质量水平的领域都会越来越成立。而这些恰恰是当今最有价值的知识工作领域之一。

我们的大脑并不适应即将进入的新场景：算力成为我们可以弹性调节以解决任何想解决问题的变量。我们习惯于认为投入一项任务的时间与产出相关。大多数知识工作或科学领域一直是这种相关关系。这正在永远改变。

原文：We’re moving from an era of AI where the output comes roughly at the rate that you can chat back and forth with the AI to work with you, to a period where you can set a problem for a set of agents to go off and do an arbitrarily large amount of work for you. This means productivity gains that are maybe 20-50% to gains that don’t really have an upper limit.

Agent swarms and the ability to fan out complex work across many agents doing tasks in parallel provide a massive unlock in a lot of spaces. This will lead to qualitatively different outcomes in my fields.

This has already been seen across the coding domain. Next, cyber is almost entirely a problem space where more agents reviewing more things will lead to far better outcomes. We’re seeing the same now in many scientific fields, and this almost certainly will change life sciences.

But this will increasingly be true in any field where you can parallelize computer-based work to complete something faster or do something at a totally different quality level. Which is actually are some of the most valuable domains of knowledge work today.

Our brains aren’t really wired for the new scenario we’re heading into where compute becomes the variable that we can flex to solve any problem that we want. We’re used to thinking that the amount of time that goes into a task is correlated to the output that we get from it. And most domains of knowledge work or science have generally always been correlated with this. That’s changing forever.引用原帖（@gdb）：frontier models, with lots of test time compute, are becoming the engine of scientific discovery 链接

【AI新闻/测评】

@teortaxesTex（保留 21 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 110 条）

2026-11-09 09:14 北京时间 ｜ Source: @teortaxesTex ｜ 原帖链接

我对“黄氏事件”（双关）的冷静看法是：国安部（MSS）几乎以最优方式下了这盘棋，比整个北京都下得好，而且不只是事后看来偶然如此。以下是我的思路。

中国人在根据实际情况校准自己的行动。2024–2025 年是一场调查：在特朗普——一个始终如一的交易型商人、交易狂和讨价还价者、最尊重骨气和蛮力的人——治下，美国在出口管制上到底愿意让步多少。为此，他们对“解放日”进行了强硬报复，拒绝了 H20 和 B30，用商务部（MOFCOM）的威胁回应 BIS 50% 规则，用切断欧洲供应回应安世半导体被没收，最终达成了釜山协议（2025 年 10 月），并放出了他们 EUV 光源进展的消息（路透 12 月公开）。随后特朗普带着 Jensen（同情中国、急于在中国市场永久关闭前重返）以及一众工业/金融 CEO 前往北京；习也去了华盛顿；诸如此类。中国人在他们所能期望的最合作的政府治下实现了最大程度的缓和。

而他们得到的只是一张每年最多 75 万颗 H200、每个实体 7.5 万颗（按传闻价格约 170 亿美元算力）的许可，借口是给特朗普多一点关税收入；外加对海外建设或小规模走私的一些宽容。鹰派用狗屁图表把这说成灾难，好像那些芯片具有战略意义，但实际上，这是对中国示好的礼貌拒绝。华为自己就能做出 H200 级别的系统；在集群层面，950DT 可以说更好。这最多相当于他们当前吞吐量几个月的量。它不会改变局面，也无法把他们从严重的供应短缺中解救出来。

而到 3 月，我认为北京也清楚了共和党很可能会在中期选举中惨败，因为它为了以色列的地缘政治需要而放弃了国内控制；当时我还不清楚，但他们一定对伊朗的韧性和五角大楼的非理性有更好的情报。于是，民主党会回来。而民主党 a）对 AI 化的科技右翼存在负面极化，Jensen 是这群人的造王者，特朗普是其靠山；b）有能力正当地协调国际力量对抗“不自由国家”；c）教条式地致力于“这就是毁灭的样子！！”式的对华发展的极端进攻，尽管今天的中国不同于 2018 年甚至 2022 年的中国，非常有能力报复。此外，他们与末日论者/EA 社区以及 Dario 那一派出口管制倡导者关系融洽。特朗普仍然可以通过行政令统治，但他不得不节约使用这一权力，而且他有许多更大的优先事项。

此外，恢复算力贸易的技术论据一直在瓦解，主要是“提供 Nvidia 可以扼杀中国内部研发、让中国人保持‘上瘾’或依赖、从而受制于未来的节流和恩惠、更害怕对抗”的观点。我过去也这样论证，因为我没有观察到短期的正反馈循环：对任何顶级中国团队来说，放弃 Nvidia 的代价都太高了，尽管它很稀缺；代价以在一个非常残酷而神经质的市场中停滞数月来衡量。与美国人对计划经济的幻想相反，中共似乎不愿意或没有能力解决这个战略死亡陷阱，单靠补贴是不够的。

这里一个重要因素，同样是 DeepSeek。我仍然不确定 2025 年关于 R2 在昇腾上失败的传言有多真实（逻辑上讲，从来就没有 R2 项目），但 2025 年底到 2026 年初的大部分时间确实用于转型，最终“鲸鱼”为中国生态的结晶提供了一个晶核，构建了默认的华为-TileLang 技术栈（正如梁文锋从一开始就打算做的那样）。他们为华为的路线图创造了被锁定的需求和反馈，我现在预计这将加速并拉动二线厂商。
我不确定滞留在香港的 H200 是否能改变这一切。鉴于 DeepSeek 到目前为止一直成功地使用 CUDA，不受限制的销售可能会把这一演进推迟一年左右。但它们本来就受限制，额外的 7.5 万颗合法 H200 对“鲸鱼”打算做的事情来说……就是不够。所以，总的来说，华盛顿让这成了必然。

最后，Nvidia 对美国生态的重要性认知已被 AI 本身的进步所削弱。OpenAI 可以直接造出至少能与 Blackwell 竞争的 Jalapeno，而且是第一代；Anthropic 也会设计出很棒的芯片；TPU 很棒，Meta 和 Amazon 在进步。Broadcom 等足以维持供应链完整性。AI 可以写出超人类水平的 kernel（梁文锋也提出过这一点），照这个速度，CUDA 护城河将无法持续，除了一些定制的业余实验（每一个严肃的实验室现在都有能力为其大规模工作负载构建更好的技术栈）。最重要的是，Rubin 的爬坡比预期慢、问题重重，而且令人担忧地依赖中国供应商。

而且必须认识到，鲁比奥关于黄氏的备忘录早在一年多以前就起草了。事情不是“需要一段时间”，而是需要等到行动变得合宜为止。在第 47 任总统前两年的轨迹下，黄氏行动成本相对较低。到 2026 年中，迹象已经很清楚：在下一代 Nvidia 硬件广泛供货之前，打压就会到来。

那么，理性的做法是什么？我认为是继续榨取 Jensen、收集情报、让企业利用海外算力满足当前运营开支、为无法在国内大规模生产的组件以及真正能带来质变的系统（目前是 NVL72 Blackwell）提供走私便利，并迅速发展国内供应链。

我认为 MSS 下得很好。这可能仍然不够，但我不确定他们还能做得更好多少。

原文：My cold take on the Huangs Affair (pun intended) is that the MSS has played this near-optimally, better than Beijing writ large, and not just accidentally-in-retrospect. Here is my thinking.

The Chinese are calibrating their moves to facts on the ground. 2024-2025 was an investigation into just how much the US is willing to budge on export controls under Trump, who is a consistently transactional merchant, a deal junkie and haggler respecting backbone and brute force above all. To this end they've retaliated harshly to Liberation Day, refused H20s and B30s, responded to BIS 50% with the MOFCOM threat and to Nexperia seizure with cutting off European supply, secured an eventual Busan deal (Oct 2025), and leaked news on their EUV light source progress (made public by Reuters in Dec). Then Trump went to Beijing with Jensen (China-sympathetic and desperately interested in getting back into their market before it closes forever) at his side and industrial/finance CEOs in tow; and Xi went to Washington; and so on and so forth. The Chinese have achieved the maximum rapproachment possible under the most cooperative administration they could hope for.

And all they've got for it was a permit for max 750K H200s per year, 75K per entity (about $17B worth of compute at alleged prices), under the pretext of marginally more tariff bux for Trump; plus some lenience with regard to overseas buildouts or small-scale smuggling. Hawks catastrophised about that with bullshit charts where those chips would be strategically significant, but really, this was a polite rejection of Chinese advances. Huawei can make H200-grade systems themselves; on a cluster level, 950DTs are arguably better. It's worth at most a few months of their current throughput. It wouldn't have moved the needle or rescued then from crippling supply scarcity.

And by March, I think it also became clear to Beijing that the GOP will most likely crushingly lose the midterms, having abandoned domestic control in favor of Israeli geopolitical needs; it wasn't then clear to me, but they must have had better intel about Iranian resilience and Pentagon's irrationality. Thus, Democrats would have come back. And Democrats are a) negatively polarized against the AI-pilled tech right, of which Jensen is the kingmaker and Trump the patron; b) capable of legitimately coordinating international effort against Unfree Countries; c) dogmatically committed to "this is what annihilation looks like!!" turbo-aggression against Chinese development, even though the current China, unlike the China of 2018 or even 2022, is very capable of retaliation. Plus they have rapport with the doomer/EA community and the Dario side of export control advocacy. Trump can still rule by EOs, but he'll have to ration this power, and he has many larger priorities.

Additionally, technical arguments for resuming compute trade have been unraveling, chiefly the idea that availability of Nvidia can strangle internal R&D and keep the Chinese "addicted" or dependent, thus subject to future throttling and treats and more afraid of confrontation. I used to argue this too, because I didn't observe a near-term positive feedback loop: it was too costly for any top-tier Chinese group to switch away from Nvidia, scarce though it is; the cost measured in months of stagnation in a very cutthroat and neurotic market. The CCP, contra American fantasies about planned economy, seemed unwilling or incapable to address this strategic death trap, and mere subsidies wouldn't have sufficed.

A big factor here is, again, DeepSeek. I am still not sure how legit the rumors about the failed R2-on-Ascend in 2025 were (logicallt speaking, there never was the R2 project), but much of late 2025-early 2026 was indeed dedicated to transition, and in the end the Whale has provided a nucleus for crystallization of the Chinese ecosystem, building the default Huawei-TileLang stack (as Wenfeng intended to do from the start). They create captive demand and feedback for Huawei's roadmap, which I now expect to accelerate and pull up second-fiddle players too.
I'm not sure if H200s stranded in Hong Kong could have changed any of it. Given how successfully DeepSeek had been using CUDA until now, it is possible that unrestricted sales would have postponed this evolution for another year or so. But they were restricted anyway, and extra 75K legal H200s are just… not enough for what Whale intends to do. So, on balance, Washington had made this an inevitability.

Finally, Nvidia's perceived importance for the American ecosystem has been undermined by the very progress in AI. OpenAI can just up and make Jalapeno that is at the very least competitive with Blackwell, at Gen 1; Anthropic will also design a great chip; TPUs are awesome, Meta and Amazon are improving. Broadcom et al. are enough to maintain the supply chain integrity. AIs can write superhuman kernels (Wenfeng also makes this point) and at this rate the CUDA moat will not last except for bespoke hobbyist experiments (every serious lab is now capable of building a better stack for their large scale workloads). On top of it all, Rubin ramp is slower than expected, troubled, and worryingly dependent on Chinese suppliers.

And it is important to realize that Rubio's memo on Huangs had been drafted well over a year ago. Things don't just "take a while", they take as long as it takes for the action to become expedient. Under the trajectory of the first two years of 47th, the Huang operation was relatively costless. By mid-2026, the writing was on the wall that the crackdown is coming before the next generation of Nvidia hardware is broadly available.

What, then, was the rational move? I think it was to keep milking Jensen, collecting intel, letting companies utilize overseas compute for current opex, facilitating smuggling of components that can't be produced at scale domestically and systems that actually make some qualitative difference (NVL72 Blackwells right now), and rapidly developing domestic supply chain.

I think the MSS played this very well. It's probably still not enough, but I'm not sure they could have done much better.引用原帖（@jakehalloran1）：My hot take is MSS played this disastrously wrong. Jensen doesn’t need to be honeypotted to want to sell gpus to China. And now that he got caught there’s gonna be no more gpus to China. Or anywhere. Lmao 链接

我不明白为什么人们对 DSH（DeepSeek Harness）大惊小怪
154 万 DAU 只是 DeepSeek DAU 的一小部分。如果它目前每天处理 92T token，那仅是 19 天前 DeepInfer 开源时其单个推理集群处理量的 59%；根据 V4.1F 的减速和我对其机队动态的估算，这不到其当前吞吐量的四分之一，即使不算第三方提供商。而模型与 harness 协同设计的承诺显然足以吸引重度用户，他们消耗的 token 比普通用户高几个数量级。
此外，它就是一个适用于任何 LLM 的好用 harness，一个易于调整的预打包应用。
不过我确实欣赏它如何彻底摧毁了使用各种“第一方” Claude Code/Codex 仿制品和第一代 agent IDE 的理由。命运眷顾勇者，中国人。DSH 会变得更大。事实上，它最终的 1.0 版本很可能成为 agentic 时代的默认开放操作系统。

原文：I don't see why people are freaking out about DSH
1.54M DAU is a sliver of DeepSeek's DAU. If it's currently processing 92T tokens daily, that's just 59% of what their one inference cluster did at the point of DeepInfer open sourcing, 19 days ago; judging by V4.1F's slowdown and my guesstimates of their fleet dynamics, it's less than a quarter of their current throughput, even ignoring third party providers. And the promise of model-harness codesign is obviously enough to lure the power users, who consume OOMs more tokens than the average.
Plus it's just a neat harness for any LLM, a pre-packaged app that is easy to tweak.
I do however appreciate how it's totally wiping out the argument for using various "first party" Claude Code/Codex me-toos and first-generation agent IDEs. Fortune favours the bold, Chinaman. DSH will grow larger. Indeed, its eventual 1.0 release might well become the default open OS for the agentic era.引用原帖（@business）：DeepSeek, the Chinese AI disruptor, released striking adoption numbers for its AI agent infrastructure as it continues to challenge Anthropic and OpenAI with an entire swath of applications built around open-source AI technologies https://t.co/rt0fo8UsLU 链接

2026-11-09 13:21 北京时间 ｜ Source: @teortaxesTex ｜ 原帖链接

（回复）> 我认为服务效率意味着在可预见的未来权重将保持静态。

我认为这在相当程度上是错的。主要是，我认为企业级在 agent 上的支出（以及你在这里假设的支出）足以支撑专用部署。想想 DeepSeek-superagent，或我听说的网络安全研究，它们通宵运行 5 轮、每轮 200–1000 个 agent 的集群。OpenAI 对 NS（Navier–Stokes）的攻关规模更大。如果用集群解决的任务具有很高的经济价值，那么在这种模式下让训练回本并不需要太多技术进步，当然我们仍需解决持续学习的固有问题（固化和脆弱性、遗忘、缺乏良性遗忘、错误泛化，等等）。这大概与你所说的激励并不矛盾。

当然，这不会自动帮助面向大众提供的 GPT-X/Claude Y，但它们可以靠 .md 维基记忆凑合。

我也乐观地认为，在经过几轮（或几百、几千轮）上述过程后，与溯因相关的技能能够泛化，因为递归压缩的模式本身会被学会。Agentic RL 已经依赖压缩（compaction）（见此处的 Inkling），所以我们只需要把同样的原则扩展到把集群上下文蒸馏为权重更新。

我预计 DeepSeek 已经在朝这个方向思考

原文：> I think the efficiency of serving implies the staticness of the weights for the forseable future.

I think this is meaningfully wrong. Chiefly, I think that the enterprise level spending on agents (and the spending you assume here) justifies dedicated deployment. Consider DeepSeek-superagent, or the cyber research I've heard about, they run a sequence of 5 swarms of 200-1000 agents overnight. OpenAI's assault on NS was even larger. If tasks solved with swarms are highly economically valuable, it won't take a lot of technical progress to make training pay back in this regime, though of course we'll still need to solve inherent issues of continual learning (hardening and brittleness, forgetting, lack of benign forgetting, misgeneralization, whatever). Probably this doesn't contradict what you say about incentives.

This doesn't automatically help the GPT-X/Claude Y served to the commoners, of course, but they can make do with .md wiki memories.

I am also optimistic that abduction-relevant skills can generalize after a few (or a few hundred, or thousand) rounds of the process described above, as the pattern of recursive compression itself becomes learned. Agentic RL already relies on compaction (see Inkling https://x.com/teortaxesTex/status/2119179317846462802 here) so all we need is to extend the same principle to the distillation of swarm-contexts into weight updates.

I expect DeepSeek to think in this direction already引用原帖（@teortaxesTex）：Very interesting digest of Inkling 2S, for me this is the highest-signal part https://t.co/1YtzYhK3DB https://t.co/DY0flWUlVN 链接所回复原帖（@doomslide）：About abduction I want to clarify my view. I believe what the technology does fundamentally is the simulation of text. I believe this deeply. That's what also allows me to believe that in principle it could simulate the entire service sector. The questions I have about it are at the economic level. I think the efficiency of serving implies the staticness of the weights for the forseable future. An… 链接

2026-11-09 04:46 北京时间 ｜ Source: @teortaxesTex ｜ 原帖链接

（回复）我对任何残存的硬性障碍持怀疑态度的最大理由，是导致当下这一刻的机器学习思想史本身，这段历史以分形的方式类似于它的产出。还记得 Rosenblatt 吗？Hinton、Sutskever、Kingma、Vaswani？Mordvintsev 的 DeepDream，我个人的“啊哈”时刻？何恺明的 ResNet，哈？这其中哪一个是伟大的范式飞跃？王道不过是为联结主义理顺后勤工作。一直是连续松弛、迭代解决问题，加上一些自然科学类比，以及中上水平博学的科学家可用的应用数学工具的组合；当然还有对过于聪明的分支的修剪，以及那些在智识上野心过大的名人（如 LeCun）项目的惨烈失败。Hinton 本人曾认为他的发明是一个死胡同式的仿品，一个半聪明的研究原型，无法与大脑的真实算法相比；而他对此的信心被一个我们今天只会怜悯地一笑的模型讲的笑话击碎了。我们从未停滞在需要某种深刻的新数学或突破性概念洞见的地方——然而我们走到了这里：网络已学会熟练运用人类概念，掌握冻结的人类心智所具备的全部推理模式（不，我不同意最好的模型完全无法进行溯因推理，你之前提过这点；它们在长会话中正在摸索它，试图把表征压缩成紧凑的假设，并提出研究分支来检验）。确实缺了点什么，部分显然是由于缺乏需求，部分出于我不理解的原因。事实上，我认为最好的公开模型即使写这篇帖子都做不到，除非提供大量作者输入，你的反驳也一样。但这有必要吗？它们又快又便宜。从现在起，它们将做比创造它们时多数百倍的、性质上相当的迭代工作，只要我们付得起。而且令人难以置信的是，前期已经投入了多得离谱的资金。这会通向哪里？朴素归纳给出了答案。它们将逐渐做出越来越好的机器学习研究，开发越来越好的模型。这就是所谓的“RSI”。其确切指数有待讨论，也取决于市场激励。当持续学习得到充分解决时（在小批量上进行真正的参数学习），无论是否在人类指导下，它都会加速。我认为它会被解决。
我可以抽象地想象自己是错的，我写过这个，但实际上并不认为我们有任何可靠理由相信这趟旅程会停下或显著放缓。那将是傲慢，在我身上是不配的傲慢，而且我——也许自恋地——认为即使在那些有资格傲慢的人身上也仍然是傲慢。从感知机到 Astra 的旅程，比从 Astra 到某个 Scholze 的旅程更长。人与人差异很大，但他们并不是性质上不同的物种，尽管数学家喜欢这样想象自己。如果 LLM 在征服了数学家父母的水平之后就停下，那首先会是一个重大的人类学发现，然后才是关于 LLM 的发现，而我想我们这里并不是在做人类学。

原文：My biggest reason for skepticism about any remaining hard barriers is the intellectual history of ML itself leading to this moment, history which fractally resembles its output. Recall Rosenblatt? Hinton, Sutskever, Kingma, Vaswani? Mordvintsev's DeepDream, my personal "aha" moment? He's ResNet lol? What in all of that was a great paradigmatic leap? The King's road was just ironing out the logistics for connectionism, It's been continuous relaxation, iterative problem-solving, with some natural science analogies and combinations of tools of applied math available to above-average erudite scientists, and indeed pruning of too clever by half branches; and spectacular crashes of programs of those luminaries who grew too intellectually ambitious, like LeCun. Hinton himself assumed that his invention is a dead end facsimile, half-clever research prototype that can't measure up to the brain's actual algorithm; and his confidence in that was shattered by a joke from a model we'd only laugh at in pity today. At no point did we stall in need of some profoundly new mafs or a breaking conceptual insight – and yet we're here, where networks have learned to competently operate with human concepts, with the entire repertoire of reasoning motifs available to a frozen human mind (no I do not agree that the best models are wholly incapable of abduction, which you brought up before; they are grasping at it in long sessions, they try to condense their representations into compact hypotheses and come up with research arms to test them). Something is missing, partially clearly due to lack of demand, partially for reasons I don't understand. Indeed, I think the best public models can't even write this post unless provided with massive authored input, nor your rebuttals. But is that necessary? They are fast and cheap. From now on they will do hundreds of times as much qualitatively comparable iterative work as had been done to create them, as much as we can afford to pay. And incredibly, obscenely much had been paid upfront already. Where does this lead? Naive induction provides the answer. They will gradually do better and better ML research, developing better and better models. That is what is called "RSI". Its exact exponent is subject to debate and market incentives. When continual learning is adequately solved (with actual parametric learning on small batches), whether under human guidance or not, it will accelerate. I think it will be solved.
I can abstractly imagine being wrong, I've written on that https://t.co/BTQ7Jb32O0, but do not actually believe that we have any solid reason to believe this ride stops or meaningfully slows down. That would be hubris, and unearned hubris in my case, and I – perhaps narcissistically – believe it'd still be hubris on part of those who have earned the right to it. The journey from perceptron to Astra was longer than from Astra to some Scholze. Humans differ a lot but they are not qualitatively different species, for all that mathematicians like to imagine themselves as such. If LLMs stop after conquering the level of mathematicians' parents, that'd be a big anthropological discovery before it is a finding about LLMs, and we're not exactly doing anthropology here, I think.引用原帖（@teortaxesTex）：btw I've started having doubts about near-term transformative AGI. Timelines up to 2035 or 2040 seem plausible (my mainline scenario is ≤2028 still).

We have scant theory of human scientific genius and near zero reliable data on its inner operation. «A genius is just a https://t.co/aHFcjlVguy https://t.co/eCmsJt4fT8 链接所回复原帖（@doomslide）：@teortaxesTex It's only fitting SSI should be the first to reach that particular milestone. 链接

2026-11-08 21:55 北京时间 ｜ Source: @kimmonismus ｜ 原帖链接

我试图解释为什么这么多人对 AI 有深深的反感。我援引精神分析学创始人西格蒙德·弗洛伊德。

弗洛伊德在 1917 年的《精神分析的一个困难》中提出了一个深刻的论点。他写道：“人类的普遍自恋、人类的自爱，迄今为止已经因科学研究而遭受了三次严重的打击。”

这三次打击是指：

1）宇宙论的颠覆——哥白尼：地球不是宇宙的中心。因此，人类不能再把自己的居所视为理所当然的世界中心。

2）生物学的打击——达尔文：人类来自动物界，与其他生物有亲缘关系。人在生物学上并不在自然之外。

3）心理学的打击——精神分析（弗洛伊德本人）：人类无法完全触及自己的心灵生活，也无法完全控制自己的冲动。据弗洛伊德说，推动他的许多东西对他自己来说仍是无意识的。

对于第三次打击，弗洛伊德找到了一个特别令人难忘的表述。在 1916/17 年的《精神分析引论》中，他写道自我“甚至不是自己家里的主人”。他在那里还称之为“第三次也是最敏感的打击”。

借鉴弗洛伊德，我断言我们正在经历第四次自恋打击、第四次严重伤害：

人类（不得不）意识到自己不再是地球上最聪明的存在。长期以来，人们一直认为人类优于所有其他动物、在智力上无与伦比。然而，人类现在创造了一个在总体上智力超越自己的智能实体。

人类未解决的问题，例如数学中的问题，被 AI 在短时间内解决，而且发展的尽头还看不到。几乎是一种巴甫洛夫式的反射让许多人反应说：“AI 至少没有人类那么有创造力，而且永远不可能有。”他们大错特错。这也是一种心理防御机制。

因此，承认创造了某种把人类从智力王座上推下去的东西，就是我借鉴弗洛伊德所提出的第四次巨大不适。

也欢迎讨论或提出异议。

原文：I am attempting to explain why so many people experience a profound aversion to AI. I am referring to Sigmund Freud, the founder of psychoanalysis.

Freud developed a profound argument in "A Difficulty of Psychoanalysis" of 1917. In it, he writes, "that the general narcissism, the self-love of mankind, has so far experienced three severe impairments on the part of scientific research."

With these three illnesses he meant:

1) The cosmological upheaval – Copernicus: The Earth is not the center of the universe. Therefore, man can no longer consider his place of residence as the self-evident center of the world.

2) The biological illness – Darwin: Man comes from the animal kingdom and is related to other living organisms. He is not biologically outside of nature.

3) The psychological illness – psychoanalysis (Freud himself): The human being does not have complete access to his own soul life and does not fully control his drives. Much of what moves him remains unconscious to him, according to Freud.

For the third insult, Freud found a particularly memorable formulation. In his "Lectures on the Introduction to Psychoanalysis," also in 1916/17, he wrote that the ego is "not even master [...] in its own house." There he also called it the "third and most sensitive insult."

In reference to Sigmund Freud, I assert that we are currently experiencing the fourth narcissistic phase, the fourth severe impairment:

Humans (have to) realize that they are not the most intelligent entity on Earth any longer. For a long time, it was believed that the species of human was superior to all other animals and unparalleled in its intelligence. However, humans have now created an intelligent entity that, in general, surpasses them in intelligence.

Unresolved human problems, like those in mathematics, are resolved by AI within a short time and the end of development is not in sight. Almost a Pavlovian reflex causes many people to react by claiming that "AI is at least not as creative as humans and could never be." They are greatly mistaken. It is also a psychological defense mechanism.

The admission of having created something that pushes humans off the throne of intelligence is therefore the fourth great malaise that I postulate in reference to Freud.

Also feels invited to discuss or disagree about it.

@ArtificialAnlys（保留 7 条 / 窗口内原创及回复 7 条）

2026-11-09 07:51 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

推出 Artificial Analysis Decision Index v2：一项新的专有评测、更新的基准，以及一个奖励模型校准的核心指标

我们发布了 Artificial Analysis Decision Index 第二版，以更好地反映现实世界的实用性并防范基准刷分。该指数现在 45% 建立在私有数据集上，高于 v1 的 25%。来自 @NaceAI 的 Drex Max v1.1 以 73 分在决策模型中小幅领先，与 GLiDE v1.2（@fastinoAI）和 Mammoth 27B（@baselabs）相差不到 1 分

Decision Index v2 的更新：

➤ Decision Score：我们采用一个同时奖励正确性和校准置信度的新核心指标。每题得分 0 到 100：答对高于 50，答错低于 50，对首选答案的置信度在各自半区内调整分数

➤ AA-Paperclip（30%）：我们新增了一个包含 1500 份文档的私有基准，包括简历、收据、发票以及 AI 和机器学习研究论文。模型对每份文档做出决策并提取事实，作为单一决策共同评分，只有 15 个答案全对才算正确

➤ AA-Symmetry（15%）：评估模型是否会因措辞不同的等价问题而改变答案。我们测试 120 个问题及其变体，如重排选项、交换标签和否定的是/否问题，然后衡量模型概率在这些变体间的一致性

➤ AA-Omniscience-Decision v1.1 和 HLE-MultiChoice v1.1（各 15%，从 25% 下调）：AA-Omniscience-Decision 现在在十种表述中变换其“我不知道”选项的措辞。HLE-MultiChoice 增加了一个使用同样十种表述的拒答选项

我们还加入了 EvalSafe-ONET-AA（15%），即我们对 @typesafeai 的 ONET 基准的实现。我们保留了 @NVIDIAAI 的 When2Call，权重下调至 10%（从 25%），并因饱和度过高移除了 WildGuardTest

主要结果：

➤ 指数顶部三个决策模型相差不到 1 分：Nace AI 的 Drex Max v1.1 和 Fastino Labs 的 GLiDE v1.2 均为 73 分，Base Labs 的 Mammoth 27B 为 72 分

➤ 决策模型每 100 万任务成本相差 60 倍以上：Celeris-1.1 Decision（14 美元）、Decider v1.2（68 美元）和 Drex Max v1.1（102 美元）位于决策指数-成本帕累托前沿，而 Clef 成本为 859 美元。token 价格不是唯一的成本驱动因素：包括 Drex Max v1.1 和 Decider v1.2 在内的大多数决策模型对一份文档的所有问题只计一次输入费用，而 GLiDE v1.2 和 Decider Flash v1.2 每个问题都计费。这意味着 GLiDE v1.2 成本为 780 美元，尽管标价只高 33%，却是 Drex Max v1.1 的近 8 倍

➤ 语言模型可以得分更高，但每任务成本和时间也更高：Claude Haiku 5.5（Max）在 Decision Index v2 上得 77 分，GPT-6 Luna（Max）得 76 分，比 Drex Max v1.1 高约 3 分。GPT-6 Luna（Max）每 100 万任务成本 1530 美元，而 Drex Max v1.1 为 102 美元（15 倍），每任务耗时 11.34 秒对 275 毫秒（约慢 40 倍）

原文：Introducing the Artificial Analysis Decision Index v2: a new proprietary evaluation, updated benchmarks, and a headline metric that rewards model calibration

We’ve released the second version of the Artificial Analysis Decision Index to better reflect real-world utility and guard against benchmark gaming. 45% of the Index is now built on private datasets, up from 25% in v1. Drex Max v1.1 from @NaceAI narrowly leads among decision models at 73, within 1 point of GLiDE v1.2 (@fastinoAI) and Mammoth 27B (@baselabs)

Updates in Decision Index v2:

➤ Decision Score: We adopt a new headline metric that rewards a model for both correctness and calibrated confidence. Each question scores from 0 to 100: right answers score above 50, wrong answers below 50, and confidence in its top answer moves the score within each half

➤ AA-Paperclip (30%): We added a new private benchmark of 1,500 documents including resumes, receipts, invoices, and AI and machine learning research papers. Models make decisions and extract facts from each document and are scored jointly as a single decision, correct only if all 15 answers are right

➤ AA-Symmetry (15%): This evaluates whether models change their answers for equivalent questions worded differently. We test 120 questions with variations such as reordered options, swapped labels, and negated yes/no questions, then measure how consistent the model’s probabilities are across those variations

➤ AA-Omniscience-Decision v1.1 and HLE-MultiChoice v1.1 (15% each, down from 25%): AA-Omniscience-Decision now varies the wording of its “I don’t know” option across ten alternatives. HLE-MultiChoice adds an option to decline using the same ten phrasings

We’ve also added EvalSafe-ONET-AA (15%), our implementation of @typesafeai’s ONET benchmark. We have retained When2Call from @NVIDIAAI in the Index at a decreased weighting of 10% (down from 25%), and removed WildGuardTest given its high saturation

Key results:

➤ Three decision models sit within 1 point at the top of the Index: Drex Max v1.1 from Nace AI and GLiDE v1.2 from Fastino Labs both score 73, with Mammoth 27B from Base Labs scoring 72

➤ Cost per 1M Tasks varies by more than 60x across decision models: Celeris-1.1 Decision ($14), Decider v1.2 ($68) and Drex Max v1.1 ($102) sit on the Decision Index vs. Cost Pareto frontier, while Clef costs $859. Token price is not the only cost driver: most decision models, including Drex Max v1.1 and Decider v1.2, bill a document’s input once for all its questions, while GLiDE v1.2 and Decider Flash v1.2 bill it for every question. This means GLiDE v1.2 costs $780, nearly 8x higher than Drex Max v1.1 despite only 33% higher list prices

➤ Language models can score higher, alongside higher Cost and Time per Task: Claude Haiku 5.5 (Max) scores 77 and GPT-6 Luna (Max) scores 76 on Decision Index v2, putting them ~3 points ahead of Drex Max v1.1. GPT-6 Luna (Max) costs $1,530 per 1M Tasks vs. $102 for Drex Max v1.1 (15x the cost) and takes 11.34 seconds per task vs. 275 milliseconds (~40x slower)

2026-11-09 07:51 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

Decision Score 同时奖励模型的正确答案和良好校准的置信度。决策模型的答案通常在其置信度超过阈值时被采纳；否则交给人或更强的模型。Decision Score 衡量所有阈值下的平均收益，因此不依赖于选择单一截断点。

对每个问题，得分为 50 × (1 + c − (p − c)²)，其中 p 是模型赋予所选答案的概率，c 是答案是否正确的标记（正确 c = 1，错误 c = 0）。在整个评测中，这等于 50 × (1 + 准确率 − Brier 分数)。

纳入置信度会影响整体排行榜排名。在 AA-Paperclip 上，就原始准确率而言，Clef 通过 36% 的文档，Celeris-1.1 Decision 通过 24%，但由于校准更好（Brier 分数 0.15 对 Clef 的 0.27），Celeris-1.1 Decision 在 Decision Score 上与其持平。在整个指数中，GLiDE v1.2 在准确率指标上领先 Drex Max v1.1（60 对 58），而 Drex Max v1.1 在 Decision Index 上略胜一筹。

原文：The Decision Score rewards models for both correct answers and well-calibrated confidence. A decision model’s answer is typically acted on when its confidence clears a threshold; otherwise, it is passed to a person or a more capable model. The Decision Score measures the average payoff across all thresholds, so it does not depend on choosing a single cutoff.

For each question, the score is 50 × (1 + c − (p − c)²), where p is the probability the model assigns to its chosen answer and c is a flag for whether the answer was correct (c = 1 if correct, c = 0 if incorrect). Across an evaluation, this is equal to 50 × (1 + Accuracy − Brier score).

Including confidence impacts overall leaderboard rankings. On AA-Paperclip, for raw accuracy Clef passes 36% of documents and Celeris-1.1 Decision passes 24%, but Celeris-1.1 Decision matches it on the Decision Score due to better calibration (Brier score of 0.15 vs. 0.27 for Clef). Across the Index, GLiDE v1.2 leads Drex Max v1.1 on accuracy metrics (60 vs. 58), while Drex Max v1.1 edges ahead on the Decision Index.所回复原帖（@ArtificialAnlys）：Introducing the Artificial Analysis Decision Index v2: a new proprietary evaluation, updated benchmarks, and a headline metric that rewards model calibration

We’ve released the second version of the Artificial Analysis Decision Index to better reflect real-world utility and guard against benchmark gaming. 45% of the Index is now built on private datasets, up from 25% in v1. Drex Max v1.1 from @Na… 链接

2026-11-09 07:51 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

AA-Paperclip 测试模型在真实文档上提取事实和做出决策的能力。其 1500 份私有文档每份有五个决策和十个待提取事实。关键指标衡量模型 15 个问题全部答对的文档比例。对于 Decision Score，模型对一份文档的置信度是其 15 个答案概率的乘积。

Claude Haiku 5.5（Max）通过 92% 的文档，GPT-6 Luna（Max）通过 86%。Drex Max v1.1（63%）和 GLiDE v1.2（62%）领先决策模型，其后是 Decider v1.2（55%）。全部通过的要求把在单个答案上看起来接近的模型区分开来。Drex Max v1.1 答对 97% 的答案，接近 Claude Haiku 5.5（Max）的 99%，但文档通过率为 63% 对 Haiku 的 92%。决策模型丢失的文档大多在决策而非提取上：Drex Max v1.1 在 71% 的文档上五个决策全对，在 88% 的文档上十项提取全对。

原文：AA-Paperclip tests models on extracting facts and making decisions about real-world documents. Each of its 1,500 private documents has five decisions and ten facts to extract. The key metric measures the share of documents where a model answers all 15 questions correctly. For the Decision Score, the model’s confidence in a document is the product of its 15 answer probabilities.

Claude Haiku 5.5 (Max) passes 92% of documents and GPT-6 Luna (Max) passes 86%. Drex Max v1.1 (63%) and GLiDE v1.2 (62%) lead the decision models, followed by Decider v1.2 (55%). The all-pass requirement separates models that look close on individual answers. Drex Max v1.1 gets 97% of answers right, close to Claude Haiku 5.5 (Max) at 99%, but passes 63% of documents against Haiku's 92%. Decision models lose most documents on the decisions rather than extraction, with Drex Max v1.1 correctly making all five decisions on 71% of documents and all ten extractions on 88%.所回复原帖（@ArtificialAnlys）：The Decision Score rewards models for both correct answers and well-calibrated confidence. A decision model’s answer is typically acted on when its confidence clears a threshold; otherwise, it is passed to a person or a more capable model. The Decision Score measures the average payoff across all thresholds, so it does not depend on choosing a single cutoff.

For each question, the score is 50 × (… 链接

2026-11-09 07:51 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

决策模型的成本从 Celeris-1.1 Decision 的每 100 万任务 14 美元到 Clef 的 859 美元不等。Celeris-1.1 Decision、Decider v1.2 和 Drex Max v1.1 位于决策指数-成本帕累托前沿，GPT-6 Luna（Max）（1530 美元）和 Claude Haiku 5.5（Max）（6240 美元）以更高得分延伸了这一前沿。

token 价格不是决策模型成本的唯一因素。GLiDE v1.2 和 Decider Flash v1.2 对每个问题单独评估并每次计入输入费用，因此一个 AA-Paperclip 任务使用约 6.3 万输入 token，而 Drex Max v1.1 约为 9 千。结果是 GLiDE v1.2 以每 100 万输入 token 0.04 美元的价格，每 100 万任务成本为 780 美元；Decider Flash v1.2 以 Decider v1.2 一半的 token 价格，成本为 196 美元，是后者的近 3 倍。

原文：Decision model costs ranged from $14 per 1M Tasks for Celeris-1.1 Decision to $859 for Clef. Celeris-1.1 Decision, Decider v1.2 and Drex Max v1.1 sit on the Decision Index vs. Cost Pareto frontier, with GPT-6 Luna (Max) ($1,530) and Claude Haiku 5.5 (Max) ($6,240) extending it at higher scores.

Token price isn’t the only factor in decision model cost. GLiDE v1.2 and Decider Flash v1.2 evaluate each question in a separate pass and bill the input each time, so an AA-Paperclip task uses about 63k input tokens, against about 9k for Drex Max v1.1. As a result GLiDE v1.2 costs $780 per 1M Tasks at $0.04 per 1M input tokens, and Decider Flash v1.2 costs $196, nearly 3x Decider v1.2, at half its token price.所回复原帖（@ArtificialAnlys）：AA-Symmetry measures how consistent models are when asked equivalent questions. We take a fixed task set and make changes like reordering options, swapping labels, or negating yes/no questions. AA-Symmetry includes 120 base questions and total 440 variations, testing consistency in both the chosen answer and its probability.

2026-11-09 07:51 北京时间 ｜ Source: @ArtificialAnlys ｜ 原帖链接

AA-Symmetry 衡量模型在被问及等价问题时的一致性。我们取一个固定任务集并做出改动，如重排选项、交换标签或否定是/否问题。AA-Symmetry 包含 120 个基础问题和共 440 个变体，测试所选答案及其概率两方面的一致性。

来自 @baselabs 的模型在 AA-Symmetry 上领先，原因是高拒答率。它们在各种问题变体中始终以高置信度承认不知道答案，从而获得高一致性分数。Mammoth 27B 在 AA-Symmetry 上得 96 分，Elephant 9B 得 93 分。Mammoth 27B 在 99% 的问题上跨变体选择相同答案，Drex Max v1.1 为 91%，GLiDE v1.2 为 88%，Clef 为 67%。

原文：AA-Symmetry measures how consistent models are when asked equivalent questions. We take a fixed task set and make changes like reordering options, swapping labels, or negating yes/no questions. AA-Symmetry includes 120 base questions and total 440 variations, testing consistency in both the chosen answer and its probability.

Models from @baselabs lead on AA-Symmetry, driven by a high abstention rate. They consistently admit they do not know an answer with high confidence across question variations, earning high consistency scores. Mammoth 27B scores 96 and Elephant 9B scores 93 on AA-Symmetry. Mammoth 27B selects the same answer across variations on 99% of questions, compared with 91% for Drex Max v1.1, 88% for GLiDE v1.2 and 67% for Clef.所回复原帖（@ArtificialAnlys）：AA-Paperclip tests models on extracting facts and making decisions about real-world documents. Each of its 1,500 private documents has five decisions and ten facts to extract. The key metric measures the share of documents where a model answers all 15 questions correctly. For the Decision Score, the model’s confidence in a document is the product of its 15 answer probabilities.

本周的 Paper Club 聚焦 AI 生物学。

测序成本已经崩塌，生物学产生的数据多到研究人员无法理解，而模型正开始追上。现在的机会是从读取生物数据转向对生命系统进行建模和设计。

我们汇集了在癌症基因组学、虚拟细胞、基因组语言模型和生物设计等领域工作的研究者和构建者，展示这一转变在实践中是什么样子。

我们从单个患者开始，探讨 AI 如何围绕其癌症构建一个研究项目，然后放大到整个细胞和基因组。过程中，我们深入观察一个在 DNA 上训练的语言模型，探索上下文学习是如何在模型从未被明确训练的情况下涌现的。

00:00 - 图灵测试解决了吗？一次现场实验

1:09:57 - Divya Nori：用于生物设计的少样本提示

原文：This week’s Paper Club is all about AI for biology.

Sequencing costs have collapsed, biology is generating more data than researchers can make sense of, and the models are beginning to catch up. The opportunity now is to move from reading biological data to modeling and designing living systems.

We brought together researchers and builders working across cancer genomics, virtual cells, genome language models, and biological design to show what that shift looks like in practice.

We start with a single patient, asking how AI can build a research program around their cancer, then zoom out to whole cells and genomes. Along the way, we look inside a language model trained on DNA and explore how in-context learning emerged even though the model was never explicitly trained to do it.
